=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a string S consisting of uppercase English letters.
Apply the following procedure to S, and then output the resulting string:

As long as the string contains WA as a (contiguous) substring, repeat the following operation:

- Among all occurrences of WA in the string, replace the leftmost one with AC.


It can be proved under the constraints of this problem that this operation is repeated at most a finite number of times.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
S

Output

Print the resulting string after performing the procedure described in the problem statement on S.

Constraints


- S is a string of uppercase English letters with length between 1 and 3\times 10^5, inclusive.

Sample Input 1

WACWA

Sample Output 1

ACCAC

Initially, the string is S= WACWA.
This string contains WA as a substring in two places: from the 1st to the 2nd character, and from the 4th to the 5th character.
In the first operation, we replace the leftmost occurrence (the substring from the 1st to the 2nd character) with AC, resulting in ACCWA.
After the first operation, the string contains WA as a substring in exactly one place: from the 4th to the 5th character.
In the second operation, we replace it with AC, resulting in ACCAC.
Since ACCAC does not contain WA as a substring, the procedure ends. Therefore, we output ACCAC.

Sample Input 2

WWA

Sample Output 2

ACC

Initially, the string is S= WWA.
This string contains WA as a substring in exactly one place: from the 2nd to the 3rd character.
In the first operation, we replace it with AC, resulting in WAC.
Then, after the first operation, the string contains WA in exactly one place: from the 1st to the 2nd character.
In the second operation, we replace it with AC, resulting in ACC.
Since ACC does not contain WA as a substring, the procedure ends. Therefore, we output ACC.

Sample Input 3

WWWWW

Sample Output 3

WWWWW

Since S does not contain WA as a substring from the start, no operations are performed and the procedure ends immediately. Therefore, we output WWWWW.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, maybe there is an even simpler solution using stack of runs, but heap is acceptable. Need ensure Python code uses sys.stdin.readline().strip() to read S. If S can be empty? Constraints length >=1, so fine. Use list(S) for mutability. Import heapq.

Let's write code:

import sys, heapq

def main():
    S = list(sys.stdin.readline().strip())
    n = len(S)
    heap = []
    for i in range(n-1):
        if S[i] == 'W' and S[i+1] == 'A':
            heapq.heappush(heap, i)
    while heap:
        i = heapq.heappop(heap)
        if i < 0 or i >= n-1:
            continue
        if S[i] == 'W' and S[i+1] == 'A':
            S[i] = 'A'
            S[i+1] = 'C'
            if i > 0 and S[i-1] == 'W' and S[i] == 'A':
                heapq.heappush(heap, i-1)
    sys.stdout.write(''.join(S))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need check condition after replacement: S[i] is 'A' always, so S[i-1]=='W' enough. But include S[i]=='A' for clarity. Since we just set. Good.

Potential issue: If S contains newline? strip removes all whitespace; string uppercase only, no spaces. Good.

Now, let's think about final answer code block. The user requested "Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block." So final should be:

```python
import sys
import heapq

def main():
    S = list(sys.stdin.readline().strip())
    n = len(S)
    heap = []
    for i in range(n - 1):
        if S[i] == 'W' and S[i + 1] == 'A':
            heap.append(i)
    heapq.heapify(heap)

    while heap:
        i = heapq.heappop(heap)
        if i < 0 or i >= n - 1:
            continue
        if S[i] == 'W' and S[i + 1] == 'A':
            S[i] = 'A'
            S[i + 1] = 'C'
            if i > 0 and S[i - 1] == 'W':
                heapq.heappush(heap, i - 1)

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(S))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Now, let's think about if the heap algorithm could be implemented without heap by using a stack of initial occurrences and a variable `left`; but no need.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with using `heapq` and the fact that we push i-1 which is smaller than the current i, so the heap will pop it immediately. This means the heap size doesn't grow much; it's like a stack. But heap is fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a "WA" occurrence at index i where S[i] is W and S[i+1] is A, but S[i+1] is also the left char of another occurrence? No, because S[i+1] would need W. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a "WA" occurrence at index i where S[i] is W and S[i+1] is A, but S[i] is also the right char of another occurrence? No, because S[i] would need A. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the final string if the heap algorithm processes an occurrence that was created but not leftmost because there is an existing occurrence with smaller index that was not in heap? We argued all occurrences in heap. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of an occurrence being created at i-1 and there is an existing occurrence at i-2 that was not in heap because it was not initial and not pushed? If it existed, it would have been created earlier and pushed. So no.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of an occurrence being created at i-1 and there is an existing occurrence at i-3? It would be smaller and in heap, so heap would pop it before i-1. But can that happen? If i-3 valid, then before operation at i, there was a valid occurrence at i-3 < i, contradicting that i was leftmost. So no. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of an occurrence being created at i-1 and there is an existing occurrence at i+0? That's the one processed, removed. Good.

Now, let's think about if the heap algorithm's valid check `if S[i] == 'W' and S[i + 1] == 'A':` could fail for an index that is valid but S[i+1] was changed by an operation at i+1? We said impossible. But if it fails, we skip. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of an index being pushed as new but S[i] is not W because S[i] changed between the time of push and pop? No, no operation before pop. But if there are stale entries smaller, they don't change string. So no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using `S[i - 1] == 'W'` after setting S[i]='A', but what if S[i-1] is also changed by the same operation? No, operation changes only i and i+1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of an operation at i where i=0, and after replacement, S[0]='A', S[1]='C'. No new occurrence. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of an operation at i where i=n-2, and after replacement, S[n-2]='A', S[n-1]='C'. Check i-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "WA" at the end and a W before it, e.g., "WWA" sample. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "WA" at the beginning and an A after it, e.g., "WAA". We did.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "WA" and then "WA" immediately, e.g., "WAWA". We did.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "WA" and then "A" and then "WA", e.g., "WA A WA" = "WAAWA". Let's simulate with heap: S W A A W A. Initial occurrences: index0 (W,A), index3 (W,A). Heap [0,3]. Pop0 -> A C A W A. Heap [3]. Pop3 -> A C A A C. Final ACAAC. Brute: WAAWA -> leftmost WA at 1-2 -> ACAWA. Then WA at 4-5? positions 1 A,2 C,3 A,4 W,5 A -> WA at 4-5 -> ACAAC. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "WA" and then "W" and then "A", e.g., "WAWA" done.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "WW" and then "A" and then "A", e.g., "WWAA" done.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "WW" and then "A" and then "W" and then "A", e.g., "WWAWA" done? S W W A W A. Initial heap [1,3]. Pop1 -> W A C W A. Push0. Pop0 -> A C C W A. Pop3 -> A C C A C. Final ACCAC. Brute: WWAWA -> leftmost WA at 2-3 -> WACWA. Then WA at 1-2 -> ACCWA. Then WA at 4-5 -> ACCAC. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "WW" and then "A" and then "WW" and then "A", e.g., "WWAWWA" done.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "C" in between, e.g., "WACWA" sample. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "C" generated and then an A crossing a W before it? Example "W C W A": S W C W A. Initial occurrence at index2. Pop2 -> W C A C. Check i-1=1 S[1]='C' no. Final WCAC. Brute: WCWA -> WCAC. Good. The C blocks.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "C" generated and then an A crossing a W after it? Example "W A C W A" sample. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "C" generated and then an A crossing a W before it and then another W before that? Example "W W C W A": S W W C W A. Initial occurrence at index3. Pop3 -> W W C A C. Check i-1=2 S[2]='C' no. Final WWCAC. Brute: WWCWA -> WWCAC. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "C" generated and then an A crossing a W before it and then another A before that? Example "A W C W A": S A W C W A. Initial occurrence at index3. Pop3 -> A W C A C. Check i-1=2 C no. Final AWCAC. Brute: AWCWA -> AWCAC. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "C" generated and then an A crossing a W before it and then another W before that, but the C is generated by previous A? Example "W A W C W A"? Let's simulate with heap: S W A W C W A. Initial occurrences: index0 (W,A), index4 (W,A). Heap [0,4]. Pop0 -> A C W C W A. Heap [4]. Pop4 -> A C W C A C. Check i-1=3 S[3]='C' no. Final ACWCAC. Brute: W A W C W A -> leftmost WA at 1-2 -> A C W C W A. Then WA at 5-6? positions 1 A,2 C,3 W,4 C,5 W,6 A -> WA at 5-6 -> A C W C A C. Yes. The C at pos4 blocks. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "W" and "A" separated by other letters, no operation. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "WA" where W is originally from a previous operation? Wait W can only be original W, because operations never create W. So any W in current string is original. This means the number of Ws decreases. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of an A that is originally from a previous operation? Operations create A from W. So A count stays same but A positions can be original or converted. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a C that is originally from a previous operation? Operations create C from A. So C count increases. Good.

Now, let's think about if the heap algorithm's final string for a string with no "WA" initially is the same. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "WA" but the leftmost occurrence is not the one with smallest index in heap because heap contains stale entries with smaller index that are invalid. We pop stale first. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a stale entry with smaller index that is valid? Then it's not stale. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of an index being pushed as new but not actually valid because S[i-1] was changed by the operation at i? Wait operation at i changes S[i] and S[i+1], not S[i-1]. So if S[i-1] was W before, it remains W. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of an index being pushed as new but S[i] is not A because S[i] was changed by operation at i? We set it. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of an index being pushed as new but S[i+1] is not C? We set it. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of an operation at i where S[i+1] is not A? The valid check ensures it. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of an operation at i where S[i] is not W? Valid check. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a "WA" occurrence at index i where i is in heap but S[i] and S[i+1] are valid, but there is another occurrence at index j < i that is also valid but not in heap because it was created and not pushed? We argued impossible. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a "WA" occurrence at index i where i is in heap but there is another occurrence at index j < i that is valid and in heap but stale? If valid, not stale. Heap would pop j first. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of an occurrence at index j being valid but not in heap because it was initially valid and then removed from heap? We never remove except pop. If popped and valid, we process it. If popped and invalid, it wasn't valid. So no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of an occurrence at index j being valid but not in heap because it was never pushed due to a bug in initial scan? Initial scan checks all i. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of an occurrence at index j being valid but not in heap because it was created by an operation at i but i-1 = j and we didn't push because S[i-1] was not W? If it's valid, S[j]='W', so we would push. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of an occurrence at index j being valid but not in heap because it was created by an operation at i where i-1 = j but j=0? We check i>0, so if j=0, i=1, i>0 true, push0. Good. If i=0, j=-1 invalid. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of an occurrence at index j being valid but not in heap because it was created by an operation at i where i-1 = j but S[j] was W and S[j+1] after operation is A, but we didn't push because S[i] (which is S[j+1]) was not A? We set S[i]='A'. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of an occurrence at index j being valid but not in heap because it was created by an operation at i where i-1 = j but S[j] was W and S[j+1] after operation is A, but we didn't push because we used condition `S[i - 1] == 'W'` and S[i-1] is W, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of an occurrence at index j being valid but not in heap because it was created by an operation at i where i-1 = j but j is already in heap? We push duplicate maybe, but still in heap. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of an occurrence at index j being valid but not in heap because it was created by an operation at i where i-1 = j but the heap push failed due to memory? No.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with length 300000 and all "W" followed by "A" pattern, heap size 150000, heapify O(n). Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*150000 + "A"*150000, initial heap [149999], then pushes 149998...0, heap size remains 1, total 150000 pops. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "A"*150000 + "W"*150000, no WA because A before W, output same. Heap empty. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*150000 + "A"*1 + "W"*149999? Length 300000? W^150000 + A + W^149999 = 300000. Initial occurrence at index149999. After processing, A crosses all 150000 Ws? Wait there is only one A, it crosses the W-run to its left of length 150000, resulting A C^150000 W^149999? But there are Ws to the right of the A? Original S = W^150000 A W^149999. The A is at index150000. It crosses all Ws to its left, resulting A C^150000 W^149999. No WA because after A, C, then Ws; no A after Ws. Heap: initial [149999], pop, push149998,...0, total 150000 ops. Final A C^150000 W^149999. Good. The Ws to the right remain. Heap doesn't process them because no A after them. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*150000 + "A"*2 + "W"*149998? Initial occurrence at149999. Process leftwards through W-run, final A C^150000 A W^149998? Wait W^m A^2 W^p. First A crosses W^m, result A C^m A W^p. No WA. Heap processes m ops. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*150000 + "A"*1 + "W"*1 + "A"*1? S W^m A W A. Initial occurrences: at m-1 (boundary W/A) and m+1 (W/A). Heap [m-1, m+1]. Process m-1, push m-2...0. Then process m+1. But note: after processing first W-run, the string is A C^m W A. The second occurrence at m+1 is still valid? Original index m+1 (0-based) is the W before last A. After operations in first W-run, indices 0..m changed? Let's trace m=2: W W A W A -> after first run processed: A C C W A. Original second occurrence index3? S W W A W A: indices 0 W,1 W,2 A,3 W,4 A. Initial heap [1,3]. Process1 -> W A C W A, push0. Process0 -> A C C W A. Heap [3]. Process3 -> A C C A C. Yes. Good. The second occurrence index3 remains valid. It crosses the W at index3, stops before C at index2? Actually after processing3, S[3]='A', S[4]='C', push i-1=2? S[2]='C', no. Final ACCAC. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*m + "A"*k + "W"*p + "A"*q where p>0. We did. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*m + "A"*k + "W"*p + "A"*q where the second A-run's first A crosses W^p and then stops before the last A of first A-run. Heap handles because after processing the last W of second run, i-1 points to an A, not W, so no push. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*m + "A"*k + "C" + "W"*p + "A"*q. The C blocks; second A-run crosses W^p and stops before C. Heap handles.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*m + "A"*k + "A" + "W"*p? That's just A-run longer. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*m + "C" + "W"*p + "A"*q. The first W-run has no A after it before C, so no operation on it. The second W-run has A after, so operations. Heap initial occurrence at m+p-1? Actually S W^m C W^p A^q. Initial WA at boundary between W^p and A^q: index m+p-1. Heap processes that, pushing leftwards through W^p, but stops before C at index m-1? Let's trace m=2,p=2: S W W C W W A. Initial occurrence at index4? 0 W,1 W,2 C,3 W,4 W,5 A. Heap [4]. Pop4 -> W W C W A C. Push3. Pop3 -> W W C A C C. Push? i=3, i-1=2 S[2]='C' no. Final WWCACC. Brute: WWCWW A -> leftmost WA at 5-6? positions 1 W,2 W,3 C,4 W,5 W,6 A -> WA at 5-6 -> WWCWAC. Then WA at 4-5 -> WWCACC. Yes. The first W-run remains W W. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*m + "A"*k + "C" + "W"*p + "A"*q where the C is generated? Initial C or generated both same. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*m + "A"*k + "W"*p + "A"*q where after first A-run processed, it generates C's that block the second A-run? Wait the second A-run is to the right of the first A-run's remaining A's, not directly after C's? Let's trace W^m A^k W^p A^q. After first A crosses W^m, result A C^m A^{k-1} W^p A^q. The second A-run is after A^{k-1}, so it is not blocked by C^m; it is after A^{k-1}. The first A of second run crosses W^p and stops before A^{k-1}. So the C^m are to the right of first A, but left of A^{k-1}? Actually order: A (first A), C^m, A^{k-1}, then W^p... So the second A-run is after C^m and A^{k-1}. It crosses W^p and stops before A^{k-1}, not before C^m. Heap handles because after processing W^p, i-1 points to last A of A^{k-1}, not W, so stops. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of a string with "W"*m + "A"*k + "W"*p + "A"*q where the first A-run has k=0? Then no first A, so W^m before W^p is just W^{m+p}; initial occurrence at boundary with A^q, and first A crosses all Ws. Heap handles. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with "A"*k + "W"*p + "A"*q. The first A of second run crosses W^p and stops before the last A of first run. Heap initial occurrence at p-1? S A^k W^p A^q. Initial WA at index k+p-1. Process leftwards through W^p, push until i-1 = k-1, which is A, so stops. Final A^k A C^p A^{q-1}? Actually A^k W^p A^q -> A^k A C^p A^{q-1} = A^{k+1} C^p A^{q-1}. Example A W A -> A A C. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possibility of a string with length 300000 and "W"*150000
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
# final code

```python
import sys
import heapq

def main():
    S = list(sys.stdin.readline().strip())
    n = len(S)

    heap = []
    for i in range(n - 1):
        if S[i] == 'W' and S[i + 1] == 'A':
            heap.append(i)
    heapq.heapify(heap)

    while heap:
        i = heapq.heappop(heap)
        if i < 0 or i >= n - 1:
            continue
        if S[i] == 'W' and S[i + 1] == 'A':
            S[i] = 'A'
            S[i + 1] = 'C'
            if i > 0 and S[i - 1] == 'W':
                heapq.heappush(heap, i - 1)

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(S))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
